# Mined from the sources (2026-11-23)

New duties arrive as drafts; rites the house does not keep arrive retired, with the reason and the commands against them.

- **parent → child** Reprove before you punish: try fair words first, speaking few and sober words with Scripture, in private unless the fault was open; never rail at them or make idle threats; show that you hate the sin, not the child; and follow correction with prayer for them.  
  Puritan: Christian Behaviour, 2; Puritan: Christian Behaviour, 2; Puritan: Christian Behaviour, 2; Puritan: Christian Behaviour, 2. - **parent → co_parent_in_law** Keep a betrothal once the contract is made, even if the match later seems less desirable.  
  Confucian: Yüan Ts'ai, Precepts 1.54; Korean custom: The Confucian Transformation of Korea, Korean Wedding Ceremony
- **parent → married_daughter** Keep your home open to a married daughter: welcome her back, help her when she is in need, and stand by her in childbirth.  
  Korean custom: Korean Women: View from the Inner Room, pp. 120-121; Korean custom: Kendall, Ancestors and Ghosts in Kut; Korean custom: Kendall, Ancestors and Ghosts in Kut; Korean custom: Korean Women: View from the Inner Room, pp. 120-121
- **parent → minor** In your will, name a guardian for your children while they are minors, so they are not left without care.  
  New England: Inheritance in America, PDF p. 193-195; New England: Inheritance in America, PDF p. 589-591
- **parent → son** Do not give a son in adoption lightly: never an only son, not before you can judge his nature, and once he is given, do not take him back.  
  Confucian: Yüan Ts'ai, Precepts 1.43; Confucian: Yüan Ts'ai, Precepts 1.43; Korean custom: Ancestor Worship and Korean Society, Families of the Twisongdwi Lineage 54-55; Korean custom: Korean Adoption and Inheritance, PDF p. 193-195
- **spouse → departed** Keep a season of mourning for a dead spouse before marrying again.  
  Korean custom: The Confucian Transformation of Korea, Marriage in Koryo: Institutional Aspects; Korean custom: The Confucian Transformation of Korea, Secondary Wives and Their Sons
- **step_parent → step_child** When you marry into a house with children of a former marriage, settle openly from the start their place in your house, and keep them in their own parent's inheritance: do not dispose of it, and give them their living from it.  
  English custom: Family and Inheritance, pp. 76-77; English custom: English Villagers of the Thirteenth Century, p.196-197; English custom: English Villagers of the Thirteenth Century, p. 204; English custom: English Villagers of the Thirteenth Century, Notes to Chapter XIII, n. 19; New England: Inheritance in America, PDF p. 349–351; Confucian: Yüan Ts'ai's Precepts, 1.48; Confucian: Yüan Ts'ai, Precepts I.48; Korean custom: The Confucian Transformation of Korea, Inheritance and Women  
  needs a role: children of first marriage
- **superior → inferior** Consult the younger members before deciding great matters, and let them share in managing the details.  
  Confucian: Yüan Ts'ai, Precepts I.23; Confucian: Family and Property, The Harmony of Co-Residents
- **widow → household** Live chastely in your widowhood.  
  English custom: Family and Inheritance, p. 361
- **widow → parent_in_law** After your husband's death, keep serving and caring for his parents.  
  Korean custom: The Confucian Transformation of Korea, pp. 207-209; Korean custom: The Confucian Transformation of Korea, Confucians' Immutable Image of Women
- **widow → spouse** If you marry again, marry only in the Lord.  
  Puritan: John Angell James, in Una teología de la familia, Matrimonio 4
- **wife → household** When her husband cannot provide alone, through sickness, absence or want, help support the household by her own work and means.  
  English custom: Land, Kinship and Life-Cycle, PDF pp. 437-438; New England: Inheritance in America, Changes in Rights of Women and Children 97; New England: Inheritance in America, Changes in Rights of Women and Children 97; New England: Women and the Law of Property in Early America, p. 68-69; Korean custom: Korean Women: View from the Inner Room, The Korean Shaman
- **wife → household** Work willingly and diligently with her own hands for the household, rising early, and do not eat the bread of idleness.  
  Puritan: Charles Bridges, in Una teología de la familia, Feminidad virtuosa 3; Puritan: Charles Bridges, in Una teología de la familia, Feminidad virtuosa 3; Puritan: Charles Bridges, in Una teología de la familia, Feminidad virtuosa 3; English custom: The Marriage Ring, The Husband’s Duty of Love; Korean custom: Pak Ch'o, Anti-Buddhist Memorial; Korean custom: Sin Sukchu, House Rules, Rule Six; Korean custom: Korean Women: View from the Inner Room, PDF p. 85-86

- member → clan: Report births, marriages and deaths for the family record.  
  Confucian: Kinship Organization in Late Imperial China, 1000-1940, pp. 129-131
- father → child: Watch for a child who has gone astray, and run to meet him when he turns.  
  Puritan: Charles H. Spurgeon, in Una teología de la familia, Gran perdón por grandes pecados
- kin → kin: Do not marry within the degrees of blood or marriage kinship that God's law forbids.  
  New England: A Little Commonwealth, PDF p. 130-132
- child → departed: Carry out a dead father's or mother's just wishes and settled commands.  
  English custom: Cooper, Family and Inheritance, PDF pp. 233-234

- household_head → household: Provide for his own; whoever will not has denied the faith.
